{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 78.66 78.66 L 78.66 52.99}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{-1.0}{1.0}{0.0}{56.84862pt}{38.09908pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 78.66 0 L 78.66 26.22 L 52.44 26.22}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 104.88 0 L 104.88 26.22 L 78.66 26.22}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

(a)

If
f(\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{3})=f(\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{5})=1
, then let
\mathtt{z}:=\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{3}
and
s:=1
.

(b)

If
f(\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{3})=1
and
f(\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{5})=2
, then let
\mathtt{z}:=\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{5}
and
s:=1
.

(c)

If
f(\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{3})=2
and
f(\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{5})=1
, then let
\mathtt{z}:=\prescript{}{\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}}{3}
and
s:=1
.

Finally, in each of the three cases R1–R3 the next lemma explains for which
(\mathtt{z},s)
the corresponding summand on the right-hand side of the identity in Lemma
6.4
has a non-zero factor
\zeta(p,\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z}}s))
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
\{k,\ell\}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, let
p
be any set-theoretical partition of
\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
, let
f\colon\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be any mapping and let
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
and
s^{\prime}\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be arbitrary.

(a)

If
(p,f)
is case R1,
then
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
if and only if either
|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})|=1
or both
2\leq|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})|
and
s^{\prime}=f(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})
.

(b)

If
(p,f)
is case R2 with critical data
\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}
,
then
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
if and only if either both
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}=\mathtt{z}_{1}
and
s^{\prime}=f(\mathtt{z}_{2})
or both
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}=\mathtt{z}_{2}
and
s^{\prime}=f(\mathtt{z}_{1})
.

(c)

If
(p,f)
is case R3 with critical data
(\mathsf{Z},\mathtt{z},s)
,
then
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
if and only if
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}=\mathtt{z}
and
s^{\prime}=s
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.5
, the statement
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
is equivalent to the conjunction of
p\backslash\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\cup$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\cup$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}}\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\},\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\}\backslash\{\varnothing\}\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f)
and
\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\subseteq f^{\leftarrow}(\{s^{\prime}\})
.

(a)
Because
p\backslash\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\cup$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\cup$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}}\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\},\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\}\backslash\{\varnothing\}\mathrel{\leq}p
, in the situation of

(
a
)

, where
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f)
, we only need to determine when
\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\subseteq f^{\leftarrow}(\{s^{\prime}\})
. If
|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})|=1
, that is,
\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}=\varnothing
, this condition is trivially satisfied. And if
2\leq|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})|
, then
\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\subseteq f^{\leftarrow}(\{s^{\prime}\})
holds if and only if
s^{\prime}=f(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})
because
\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\subseteq\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\subseteq f^{\leftarrow}(\{f(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\})
by assumption. That proves

(
a
)

.

(b)
In case

(
b
)

, if
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\notin\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}
, then
\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}\in p\backslash\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\cup$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\cup$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}}\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\},\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\}\backslash\{\varnothing\}
. However, because
f(\mathtt{z}_{1})\neq f(\mathtt{z}_{2})
there cannot exist any
\mathsf{B}\in\ker(f)
with
\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
. Hence,
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\notin\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}
excludes
p\backslash\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\cup$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\cup$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}}\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\},\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\}\backslash\{\varnothing\}\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f)
and thus
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
.

Hence,
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
requires the existence of
i\in\llbracket{2}\rrbracket
with
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}=\mathtt{z}_{i}
. If so, then
p\backslash\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\cup$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\cup$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}}\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\},\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\}\backslash\{\varnothing\}=p\backslash\{\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\cup$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\cup$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}}\{\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\},\{\mathtt{z}_{2}\}\}\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f)
since by assumption for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
with
\mathsf{A}\notin\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}
there exists
\mathsf{B}\in\ker(f)
with
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
. Thus, in this case,
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
is equivalent to
\{\mathtt{z}_{3-i}\}=\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}\backslash\{\mathtt{z}_{i}\}=\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\subseteq f^{\leftarrow}(\{s^{\prime}\})
, i.e., to
f(\mathtt{z}_{3-i})=s^{\prime}
, just as

(
b
)

claimed.

(c)
Finally, under the assumptions of

(
c
)

, whenever
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\notin\mathsf{Z}
, then
\mathsf{Z}\in p\backslash\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\cup$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\cup$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}}\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\},\allowbreak\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\}\backslash\{\varnothing\}\mathrel{\not\leq}\ker(f)
by the existence of
\mathtt{y}\in\mathsf{Z}\backslash\{\mathtt{z}\}\neq\varnothing
with
f(\mathtt{z})\neq s=f(\mathtt{y})
. Consequently,
p\mathrel{\not\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
if
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\notin\mathsf{Z}
.

For
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\in\mathsf{Z}
, because by assumption there is for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
with
\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{Z}=\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})
a
\mathsf{B}\in\ker(f)
with
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
the condition
p\backslash\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\cup$\cr$\displaystyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\cup$\cr$\textstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$\crcr}}}}\{\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\},\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\}\backslash\{\varnothing\}\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f)
simplifies to the existence of
\mathsf{B}\in\ker(f)
with
\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
, which is subsumed by the second condition. In other words, if
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\in\mathsf{Z}
, then
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
if and only if
\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\subseteq f^{\leftarrow}(\{s^{\prime}\})
.

If
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\neq\mathtt{z}
, then
\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\not\subseteq f^{\leftarrow}(\{s^{\prime}\})
because, by
3\leq|\mathsf{Z}|
, there exist
\mathtt{y}\in\mathsf{Z}\backslash\{\mathtt{z},\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}
with
f(\mathtt{y})=s\neq f(\mathtt{z})
by assumption. Hence,
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}}s^{\prime})
requires
\mathtt{z^{\prime}}=\mathtt{z}
. And in that case it is equivalent to
\mathsf{Z}\backslash\{\mathtt{z}\}=\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z^{\prime}})\backslash\{\mathtt{z^{\prime}}\}\subseteq f^{\leftarrow}(\{s^{\prime}\})
, which is satisfied if and only if
s^{\prime}=s
because
f(\mathtt{y})=s
for any
\mathtt{y}\in\mathsf{Z}\backslash\{\mathtt{z}\}\neq\varnothing
. Thus, the assertion of

(
c
)

is true as well and, thus, so is the claim overall.
∎

In regard of Lemmas
6.9
and
6.11
, we can now improve upon Lemma
6.4
as follows.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\{k,\ell\}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, let
\mathfrak{c}\in\{\mathnormal{\circ},\mathnormal{\bullet}\}^{\times k}
, let
\mathfrak{d}\in\{\mathnormal{\circ},\mathnormal{\bullet}\}^{\times\ell}
, let
p
be any set-theoretical partition of
\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
, let
g\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times k}
, let
j\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times\ell}
, let
r:=\mathrm{r}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(p)_{j,g}
, let

f:=g\begin{smallmatrix}\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\hskip 4.0pt\\
\hskip 4.0pt\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\end{smallmatrix}j
, let
\mathfrak{w}:=\mathfrak{c}\begin{smallmatrix}\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\hskip 4.0pt\\
\hskip 4.0pt\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\end{smallmatrix}\mathfrak{d}
, and let
x\in\mathbb{C}^{\times E}
.

(i)

If
(p,f)
is case R1, then

\displaystyle F_{r}(x)

\displaystyle{}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\\
{}\,\wedge\,|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z})|=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\quad{}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\\
{}\,\wedge\,2\leq|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z})|\end{subarray}}\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-1&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
1&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}x_{u_{f(\mathtt{z}),f(\mathtt{z})}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}}.

(ii)

If
(p,f)
is case R2 with critical data
\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}
, then

F_{r}(x)=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}_{1})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}_{1}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}_{1})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}_{1}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}+\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}_{2})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}_{2}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}_{2})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}_{2}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}.

(iii)

If
(p,f)
is case R3 with critical data
(\mathsf{Z},\mathtt{z},s)
, then

F_{r}(x)=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}.

(iv)

If
(p,f)
is case R4, then

F_{r}(x)=0
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.4
,

F_{r}(x)=\sum_{\mathtt{z}^{\prime}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}}\sum_{s^{\prime}=1}^{n}\zeta(p,\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z}^{\prime}}s^{\prime}))\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u_{s^{\prime},f(\mathtt{z}^{\prime})}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}^{\prime})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}^{\prime}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u_{f(\mathtt{z}^{\prime}),s^{\prime}}^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}^{\prime})}}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}^{\prime}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}.

From this identity, we see immediately that
F_{r}(x)\neq 0
requires the existence of
\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
and
s\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z}}s)
. Thus, Lemma
6.9
verifies

(

⁢
i
v

)

. It remains to treat the cases

(
i
)

–

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

.

(i)
In the situation of

(
i
)

, for any
\mathtt{z}^{\prime}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
and
s^{\prime}\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
we know from Lemma
6.11

(
a
)

that
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z}^{\prime}}s^{\prime})
if and only if either
|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z}^{\prime})|=1
or both
2\leq|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z}^{\prime})|
and
s^{\prime}=f(\mathtt{z}^{\prime})
. Thus, the above formula for
F_{r}(x)
simplifies to the one in

(
i
)

.

(ii)
Under the assumptions of

(

⁢
i
i

)

, Lemma
6.11

(
b
)

tells us for any
\mathtt{z}^{\prime}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
and
s^{\prime}\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
that
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z}^{\prime}}s^{\prime})
if and only if either both
\mathtt{z}^{\prime}=\mathtt{z}_{1}
and
s^{\prime}=f(\mathtt{z}_{2})
or both
\mathtt{z}^{\prime}=\mathtt{z}_{2}
and
s^{\prime}=f(\mathtt{z}_{1})
. That proves the formula for
F_{r}(x)
in

(

⁢
i
i

)

.

(iii)
Finally, if the premises of

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

are satisfied, then for any
\mathtt{z}^{\prime}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
and
s^{\prime}\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
Lemma
6.11

(
c
)

lets us infer that
p\mathrel{\leq}\ker(f\downarrow_{\mathtt{z}^{\prime}}s^{\prime})
if and only if
\mathtt{z}^{\prime}=\mathtt{z}
and
s^{\prime}=s
. In particular, at most one summand is non-zero. It follows that
F_{r}(x)
is given by the expression in

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

.
∎

6.3
Halving the number of variables

Until now we have only considered each equation in the systems from Proposition
6.2
in isolation. The next simplification will take into account that
the two-colored partitions
,
,
and
are present in any category of two-colored partitions. That fact can be used to eliminate half the variables (as, e.g., in
[
22
, Lemma 1.7]
). This is the only explicit elimination of variables that will be made in the entire proof of the main theorem.

Lemma 6.13
.

For any
g\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times 2}
and
j\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times 2}
and any
x\in\mathbb{C}^{\times E}
, if
r
is given by

(a)

\mathrm{r}^{\varnothing}_{\mathnormal{\circ}\mathnormal{\bullet}}(\hbox to7.51pt{\vbox to7.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 L 9.84 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})_{j,\varnothing}
, then
F_{r}(x)=x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{1},j_{2}}}+x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}}
.

(b)

\mathrm{r}^{\varnothing}_{\mathnormal{\bullet}\mathnormal{\circ}}(\hbox to7.51pt{\vbox to7.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 L 9.84 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})_{j,\varnothing}
, then
F_{r}(x)=x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{1},j_{2}}}+x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}}
.

(c)

\mathrm{r}^{\mathnormal{\bullet}\mathnormal{\circ}}_{\varnothing}(\hbox to7.51pt{\vbox to7.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.84 L 0 0 L 9.84 0 L 9.84 9.84}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})_{\varnothing,g}
, then
F_{r}(x)=-x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{g_{2},g_{1}}}-x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{g_{1},g_{2}}}
.

(d)

\mathrm{r}^{\mathnormal{\circ}\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{\varnothing}(\hbox to7.51pt{\vbox to7.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.84 L 0 0 L 9.84 0 L 9.84 9.84}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})_{\varnothing,g}
, then
F_{r}(x)=-x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{g_{2},g_{1}}}-x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{g_{1},g_{2}}}
.

Proof.

Only the proof of

(
a
)

is given. The others are similar.
Using Definition
4.11
, the result of Lemma
3.15
that
r=\mathrm{r}^{\varnothing}_{\mathnormal{\circ}\mathnormal{\bullet}}(\hbox to7.51pt{\vbox to7.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 L 9.84 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})_{j,\varnothing}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{1},i}u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{2},i}-\delta_{j_{1},j_{2}}1
implies

F_{r}(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{1},i}}\triangleleft u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{2},i}+u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{1},i}\triangleright x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{2},i}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\delta_{j_{2},i}\,x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{1},i}}+\delta_{j_{1},i}\,x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{2},i}})=x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{1},j_{2}}}+x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}}

because
F_{1}=0
.
∎

Notation 6.14
.

Let
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
be arbitrary.

(a)

Let
x^{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times E}
be such that for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
,

x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}}^{v}:=v_{j,i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j,i}}^{v}:=-v_{i,j}.

(b)

For any set
\mathcal{P}
of two-colored partitions
let
A(\mathcal{P},v)
denote the statement that
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
for any
r\in R_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Ultimately, it will be shown that in the case of categories of two-colored partitions the predicates
A
defined in Notation
6.14
are equivalent to those used in the formulation of the main theorem.

Proposition 6.15
.

For any category
\mathcal{C}
of two-colored partitions,
if
G
is the unitary easy compact quantum group of
(\mathcal{C},n)
,
then there exists an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\bigl(\widehat{G}\bigr)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\,\wedge\,A(\mathcal{C},v)\},}

which maps
(
the one-elemental cohomology class of
)
any
1
-cocycle
\eta
to the matrix
v
with
v_{j,i}=\eta(u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}+J_{\mathcal{C}})
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.2
, it suffices to show that the rule
x\mapsto(x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}})_{(j,i)\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times 2}}
gives a
\mathbb{C}
-linear isomorphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\times E}\,\wedge\,\forall r\in R_{\mathcal{C}}\colon F_{r}(x)=0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\,\wedge\,\forall r\in R_{\mathcal{C}}\colon F_{r}(x^{v})=0\}.}

The claimed isomorphism is well defined: Let
x\in\mathbb{C}^{\times E}
be such that
F_{r}(x)=0
for any
r\in R_{\mathcal{C}}
. Then, for any
j\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times 2}
because
\mathrm{r}^{\varnothing}_{\mathnormal{\circ}\mathnormal{\bullet}}(\hbox to7.51pt{\vbox to7.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 9.84 L 9.84 9.84 L 9.84 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})_{j,\varnothing}\in R_{\mathcal{C}}
in particular

x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{1},j_{2}}}+x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}}=0

by Lemma
6.13
, i.e.,
x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j_{2},j_{1}}}=-x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j_{1},j_{2}}}
. Hence, if we let
v:=(x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}})_{(j,i)\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times 2}}
, then for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
by definition not only
x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}}^{v}=v_{j,i}=x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}}
but also

x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j,i}}^{v}=-v_{i,j}=-x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{i,j}}=x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j,i}},

which is to say
x^{v}=x
. Thus, per assumption, in particular
F_{r}(x^{v})=F_{r}(x)=0
for any
r\in R_{\mathcal{C}}
. That proves that the map is well defined.

It is clear that the mapping is
\mathbb{C}
-linear. Moreover, it is injective because, if again
x\in\mathbb{C}^{\times E}
is such that
F_{r}(x)=0
for any
r\in R_{\mathcal{C}}
and if again
v:=(x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}})_{(j,i)\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times 2}}
, then
v=0
necessitates
x^{v}=0
by definition of
x^{v}
and thus
x=0
by the identity
x^{v}=x
established in the preceding paragraph.

To show surjectivity, we let
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
be arbitrary with
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
for any
r\in R
and abbreviate
x:=x^{v}
. Then, of course,
F_{r}(x)=0
for any
r\in R_{\mathcal{C}}
. Because moreover
x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}}=x_{u^{\mathnormal{\circ}}_{j,i}}^{v}=v_{j,i}
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
the tuple

x
is a preimage of
v
. Thus, the claim is true.
∎

The next lemma correspondingly eliminates the variables
(x_{u^{\mathnormal{\bullet}}_{j,i}})_{(j,i)\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times 2}}
from the formula obtained in Lemma
6.12
for the individual equations in the systems from Proposition
6.2
. Recall from Notation
3.1
that
f/p
denotes the quotient mapping of any mapping
f
with respect to any set-theoretical partition
p
of its domain and recall the definition of the color sum
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}
of two color tuples
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{d}
from Definition
3.4
.

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
be any two-colored partition, let
g\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times|\mathfrak{c}|}
, let
j\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times|\mathfrak{d}|}
, let
{r:=\mathrm{r}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(p)_{j,g}}
, let

f:=g\begin{smallmatrix}\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\hskip 4.0pt\\
\hskip 4.0pt\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\end{smallmatrix}j
, and let
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.

(i)

If
(p,f)
is case R1, then

\displaystyle F_{r}(x^{v})

\displaystyle{}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{A}\in p\\
{}\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1\end{subarray}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\sum_{s=1}^{n}\begin{cases}\begin{cases}v_{(f/p)(\mathsf{A}),s}&\text{if }\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=1\\
v_{s,(f/p)(\mathsf{A})}&\text{if }\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=-1\end{cases}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\quad{}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{A}\in p\\
{}\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|\end{subarray}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})v_{(f/p)(\mathsf{A}),(f/p)(\mathsf{A})}.

(ii)

If
(p,f)
is case R2 with critical data
\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}
, then

F_{r}(x^{v})={\frac{1}{2}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\})(v_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}+v_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}).

(iii)

If
(p,f)
is case R3 with critical data
(\mathsf{Z},\mathtt{z},s)
, then

F_{r}(x^{v})=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})\begin{cases}\begin{cases}v_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}&\text{if }\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})=1\\
v_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}&\text{if }\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})=-1\end{cases}\end{cases}.

(iv)

If
(p,f)
is case R4, then
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
.

Proof.

We only have to show that in each of the first three cases the right-hand sides of the identities for
F_{r}(x^{v})
in the claim agree with the corresponding ones of Lemma
6.12
. For the purposes of this proof, let
v^{\mathrm{b}(1)}:=v
and
v^{\mathrm{b}(-1)}:=v^{\mathrm{t}}
and recall
\sigma(\mathnormal{\circ})=1
and
\sigma(\mathnormal{\bullet})=-1
. Then, for any
\mathfrak{e}\in\{\mathnormal{\circ},\mathnormal{\bullet}\}
and
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
the definitions imply

x_{u^{\mathfrak{e}}_{j,i}}^{v}=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}v_{j,i}&\text{if }\mathfrak{e}=\mathnormal{\circ}\\
-v_{i,j}&\text{if }\mathfrak{e}=\mathnormal{\bullet}\end{cases}\end{cases}=\sigma(\mathfrak{e})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma(\mathfrak{e}))})_{j,i}.

If
k:=|\mathfrak{c}|
and
\ell:=|\mathfrak{d}|
and
\mathfrak{w}:=\mathfrak{c}\begin{smallmatrix}\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\hskip 4.0pt\\
\hskip 4.0pt\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\end{smallmatrix}\mathfrak{d}
, then it follows for any
\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
and any
s\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}&{}=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-{\sigma(\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}))}(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma(\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})))})_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
{\sigma(\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}))}(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma(\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})))})_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}\\
&{}=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}{\sigma(\overline{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})})}(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma(\overline{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
{\sigma(\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}))}(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma(\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})))})_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}\\
&{}=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}\end{aligned}

by the definition of the color sum and, analogously,

\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\}))})_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}.

We now distinguish the three relevant cases.

(i)
In the situation of

(
i
)

, by Lemma
6.12
the number
F_{r}(x^{v})
is given by

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\\
{}\,\wedge\,|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z})|=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\\
{}\,\wedge\,2\leq|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z})|\end{subarray}}\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{f(\mathtt{z}),f(\mathtt{z})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{f(\mathtt{z}),f(\mathtt{z})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}.

By what was shown initially, this can be rewritten identically as

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\\
{}\,\wedge\,|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z})|=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\\
{}\,\wedge\,2\leq|\pi_{p}(\mathtt{z})|\end{subarray}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}),f(\mathtt{z})}.

And that is exactly what was claimed because
\ker(f)\mathrel{\leq}p
and
\sum_{\mathtt{z}\in\mathsf{A}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})
for any
{\mathsf{A}\in p}
.

(ii)
Under the assumptions of

(

⁢
i
i

)

, Lemma
6.12
tells us that
F_{r}(x^{v})
can be computed as

\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}_{1})}_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}_{1}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}_{1})}_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}_{1}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}+\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}_{2})}_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}_{2}\in\Pi^{k}_{0}\\
x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z}_{2})}_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}_{2}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases},

which, by our initial observations, is identical to

\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}+\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{2}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{2}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}.

Since
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{i}\})\in\{-1,1\}
for each
i\in\llbracket{2}\rrbracket
, either
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\})=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{2}\})
, in which case we infer

\displaystyle F_{r}(x^{v})

\displaystyle{}=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\})((v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}+(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}),

\displaystyle{}={\frac{1}{2}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\})(v_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}+v_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}),

or
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\})=-\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{2}\})
, implying

F_{r}(x^{v})=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})}-\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(-\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}=0.

And that is precisely what we needed to show in this case.

(iii)
Finally, if the premises of

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

are satisfied, according to Lemma
6.12
and by our initial findings,

F_{r}(x^{v})=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}-x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{k}_{0},\\
x_{u^{\mathfrak{w}(\mathtt{z})}_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}}^{v}&\text{if }\mathtt{z}\in\Pi^{0}_{\ell}\end{cases}\end{cases}=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})(v^{\mathrm{b}(\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\}))})_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}.

Since this is just what we claimed, that concludes the proof.
∎

6.4
All equations of any single two-colored partition

As an intermediate step to solving the systems of linear equations of Proposition
6.15
, we now study the system of equations induced not by an entire category of two-colored partitions but only any single two-colored partition.

Definition 6.17
.

With respect to any two-colored partition
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
we say that any

v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
meets

(a)

condition P1
if for any
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{A}\in p\\
{}\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1\end{subarray}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\sum_{s=1}^{n}\begin{cases}\begin{cases}v_{h(\mathsf{A}),s}&\text{if }\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=1\\
v_{s,h(\mathsf{A})}&\text{if }\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=-1\end{cases}\end{cases}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{A}\in p\\
{}\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|\end{subarray}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{h(\mathsf{A}),h(\mathsf{A})}=0.

(b)

condition P2
if there is no
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
|\mathsf{Y}|=2
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
or if
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
.

(c)

condition P3
if there is no
\mathsf{Z}\in p
with
3\leq|\mathsf{Z}|
or if
v_{j,i}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
.

Lemma 6.18
.

For any two-colored partition
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
and any
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
, the statement
A(\{(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\},v)
is equivalent to
v
meeting simultaneously all the three conditions P1–P3 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
.

Proof.

Both implications are proved separately.

Step 1. First implication.
First, suppose that conditions P1–P3 are satisfied, let
g\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times|\mathfrak{c}|}
and
j\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times|\mathfrak{d}|}
be arbitrary and let
r:=\mathrm{r}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(p)_{j,g}
. We show that
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
. If
f:=g\begin{smallmatrix}\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\hskip 4.0pt\\
\hskip 4.0pt\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\end{smallmatrix}j
, then
{(p,f)}
falls into one of the four cases R1–R4 by Lemma
6.8

(
b
)

.

Case 1.1.

If
(p,f)
is case R1, then we can define
h:=f/p
. And, then by case

(
i
)

of Lemma
6.16
condition P1 says precisely that
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
.

Case 1.2.

Next, suppose that
(p,f)
is case R2 with critical data
\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}
. Then
F_{r}(x^{v})={\frac{1}{2}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\})(v_{f(\mathtt{z}_{1}),f(\mathtt{z}_{2})}+v_{f(\mathtt{z}_{2}),f(\mathtt{z}_{1})})
by case

(

⁢
i
i

)

of Lemma
6.16
.
Hence, if
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\})=0
we have nothing to prove. Otherwise, condition P2 guarantees that
v_{b,a}+v_{a,b}=0
for any
\{a,b\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
, thus showing
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
since
f(\mathtt{z}_{2})\neq f(\mathtt{z}_{1})
.

Case 1.3.
Now, let
(p,f)
be case R3 with critical data
(\mathsf{Z},\mathtt{z},s)
. Then condition P3 implies that
v
is diagonal. Since by case

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

of Lemma
6.16
the number
F_{r}(x^{v})
is given by
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})\,v_{f(\mathtt{z}),s}
or
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})\,v_{s,f(\mathtt{z})}
that proves
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
in this case because
f(\mathtt{z})\neq s
.

Case 1.4.
Lastly, if
(p,f)
is case R4, then
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
by case

(

⁢
i
v

)

of Lemma
6.16
. Hence, there is nothing to show.

Step 2. Second implication.
To show the converse we assume
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
for any
r\in R_{\{(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\}}
and prove that then conditions P1–P3 are met. Let
k:=|\mathfrak{c}|
and
\ell:=|\mathfrak{d}|
.

Step 2.1.
If
p=\varnothing
, condition P1 is trivially satisfied. Otherwise, for any
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
let
f:=h\circ\pi_{p}
, let
g\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times k}
and
j\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket^{\times\ell}
be such that
g\begin{smallmatrix}\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\hskip 4.0pt\\
\hskip 4.0pt\resizebox{180780}{}{$\blacksquare$}\end{smallmatrix}j:=f
and let
r:=\mathrm{r}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(p)_{j,g}
. Then
(p,f)
is case R1. Moreover,
F_{r}(x^{v})
is exactly the left-hand side of the equation in condition P1 by Lemma
6.16

(
i
)

. This proves condition P1 to be satisfied because
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
by assumption.

Step 2.2.
Now, let
\mathsf{Y}\in p
be such that
|\mathsf{Y}|=2
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
and let
\{a,b\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be arbitrary with
a\neq b
. We find
\mathtt{z}_{1}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
and
\mathtt{z}_{2}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}
such that
\mathtt{z}_{1}\neq\mathtt{z}_{2}
and
\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}=\mathsf{Y}
. If we let
f\colon\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be such that
\mathtt{z}_{1}\mapsto a
and
\mathtt{y}\mapsto b
for any
\mathtt{y}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\backslash\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\}
, then
f(\mathtt{z}_{1})\neq f(\mathtt{z}_{2})
by
a\neq b
and for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
with
\mathsf{A}\neq\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\}
there is
\mathsf{B}\in\ker(f)
with
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
, namely
\mathsf{B}=\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\backslash\{\mathtt{z}_{1}\}
. In other words,
(p,f)
is case R2. Hence,
F_{r}(x^{v})={\frac{1}{2}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\})(v_{b,a}+v_{a,b})
by Lemma
6.16

(

⁢
i
i

)

. Since
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}_{1},\mathtt{z}_{2}\})\neq 0
by assumption, condition P2 is thus met as well.

Step 2.3.
Lastly, suppose
\mathsf{Z}\in p
and
3\leq|\mathsf{Z}|
and let
\{a,b\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be arbitrary with
a\neq b
. Fix any
\mathtt{z}\in\mathsf{Z}
, let
s:=b
and define
f\colon\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
by demanding
\mathtt{z}\mapsto a
and
\mathtt{y}\mapsto b
for any
\mathtt{y}\in\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\backslash\{\mathtt{z}\}
. Then,
f(\mathtt{z})\neq s
by
a\neq b
and
f(\mathtt{y})=s
for any
\mathtt{y}\in\mathsf{Z}
with
\mathtt{y}\neq\mathtt{z}
. Moreover, for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
with
p\neq\mathsf{Z}
there exists in the shape of
\Pi^{k}_{\ell}\backslash\{\mathtt{z}\}
some
\mathsf{B}\in\ker(f)
with
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
. This means that
(p,f)
is case R3. Therefore,
F_{r}(x^{v})
is given by
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})\,v_{a,b}
or
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})\,v_{b,a}
according to Lemma
6.16

(

⁢
i
i
i

)

. Thus, by
F_{r}(x^{v})=0
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\{\mathtt{z}\})\neq 0
also condition P3 is satisfied and the proof is complete.
∎

6.5
All equations of certain special two-colored partitions

Proof.

(a)
Because

\sigma^{\varnothing}_{\mathnormal{\circ}\mathnormal{\bullet}\mathnormal{\circ}\mathnormal{\bullet}}(\mathsf{A})=0
for the only

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}

is satisfied by

if and only if

respectively

for any

is true, Lemma
6.18
implies that
v
in particular meets condition P1 with respect to
\hbox to5.2pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

and
\hbox to5.2pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.24 L 0 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

. Thus, for any
i\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
, if
h\colon\hbox to5.2pt{\vbox to7.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}
{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

is constant with value
i
, then
0=t\,\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{i,s}
respectively
0=t\,\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{s,i}
by what was said initially. By
0<t
, that proves
v
to be small.
∎

6.6
Case distinctions

The final step to proving the main theorem is upon us. According to Proposition
6.15
, it is enough to show that predicates
A
of Notation
6.14
and those of the main theorem are equivalent.

The strategy for that is the same in every case: For the category
\mathcal{C}
of two-colored partitions in question and any
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
, the statement
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to
A(\{(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\},v)
being true for any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
. By Lemma
6.18
, that is equivalent to the three conditions P1–P3 being met with respect to any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
. Thus, we only need to show that the latter is equivalent to the statement
A(\mathcal{C},v)
in the main theorem.

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be any category of two-colored partitions and
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.
If
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{O}
and

(i)

class
\mathsf{NNSB}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to the absolutely true statement.

(ii)

not class
\mathsf{NNSB}
but class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to there existing
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda I
is skew-symmetric.

(iii)

not class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to
v
being skew-symmetric.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.11

(
b
)

and

(
c
)

, the assumption that
\mathcal{C}
be case
\mathcal{O}
means
|\mathsf{B}|=2
for any
\mathsf{B}\in p
and any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
. Consequently, when checking conditions P1–P3 with respect to any given
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
there are simplifications.

\square

Condition P1 is met if and only if for any
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
,

\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})v_{h(\mathsf{A}),h(\mathsf{A})}=0.

\square

Condition P2 simplifies to the demand that
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
as soon as there is any
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
.

\square

Condition P3 is trivially satisfied and can thus be ignored entirely.

The three cases are treated individually.

(i)
If
\mathcal{C}
is class
\mathsf{NNSB}
, then all we have to show is that with respect to any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
conditions P1 and P2 are automatically satisfied. And indeed, by the initial simplification condition P1 of Lemma
6.18
is satisfied for any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and any
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
since already
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=0
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
by
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NNSB}
. Likewise, by
\mathcal{C}
being
\mathsf{NNSB}
there are no
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
, meaning condition P2 is trivially satisfied.

(ii)
The next case is that
\mathcal{C}
is not class
\mathsf{NNSB}
but still class
\mathsf{NP}
. Here, we do have to show two implications. And, we treat them separately.

Suppose that there exists
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda I
is skew-symmetric and let
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be arbitrary.
Since
v-\lambda I
is skew-symmetric
v_{j,j}=v_{i,i}
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
j\neq i
by Lemma
5.2

(
b
)

. Thus, what condition P1 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
actually demands is that the term
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{1,1}=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\,v_{1,1}
be zero, which it is since
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NP}
ensures
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=0
. Lemma
5.2

(
b
)

furthermore guarantees that
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
j\neq i
, which is why condition P2 is met, regardless of whether there is
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
or not.

Conversely, let now
A(\mathcal{C},v)
hold. By assumption, we find
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
but still
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=0
and, of course, with
|\mathsf{Y}|=2
since
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{O}
. Hence,
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
by condition P2 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
. But also, given any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
, if
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
is such that
\mathsf{Y}\mapsto j
and
\mathsf{A}\mapsto i
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p\backslash\{\mathsf{Y}\}
, then condition P1 implies
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\,v_{j,j}+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{Y}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=0
. Since
0=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{Y}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})
, i.e.,
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{Y}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=-\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})
, this means
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})(v_{j,j}-v_{i,i})=0
, which implies
v_{j,j}-v_{i,i}=0
by
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
. Hence, there exists
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda I
is skew-symmetric by Lemma
5.2

(
b
)

.

(iii)
Finally, suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is not even class
\mathsf{NP}
.

Assume that
v
is skew-symmetric and let
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be arbitrary.
Since
v
being skew-symmetric implies
v_{h(\mathsf{A}),h(\mathsf{A})}=0
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
condition P1 is met with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
. But
v
being skew-symmetric also implies
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
j\neq i
, which is why condition P2 is satisfied no matter whether there is
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
or not.

To see the converse, we assume
A(\mathcal{C},v)
. Because
\mathcal{C}
is not class
\mathsf{NP}
there exists
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
with
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\neq 0
.
For any
i\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
, if
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
is constant with value
i
, then condition P1 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
implies
0=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\,v_{i,i}
and thus
v_{i,i}=0
by
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\neq 0
. Furthermore,
since
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})
the assumption
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\neq 0
also requires the existence of at least one
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
. Hence, by the initial simplification condition P2 yields
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
j\neq i
. In other words,
v
is skew-symmetric.
∎

Proposition 6.21
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be any category of two-colored partitions and
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.
If
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{B}
and

(i)

both class
\mathsf{NNSB}
and class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to there existing
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda I
is small.

(ii)

class
\mathsf{NNSB}
but not class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to
v
being small.

(iii)

not class
\mathsf{NNSB}
but class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to there existing
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda I
is skew-symmetric and small.

(iv)

neither class
\mathsf{NNSB}
nor class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to
v
being skew-symmetric and small.

Proof.

That
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{B}
requires
|\mathsf{B}|\leq 2
for any
\mathsf{B}\in p
and any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
by Lemma
3.11

(
c
)

and, of course,
\hbox to12.31pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.15 0 C 2.15 1.19 1.19 2.15 0 2.15 C -1.19 2.15 -2.15 1.19 -2.15 0 C -2.15 -1.19 -1.19 -2.15 0 -2.15 C 1.19 -2.15 2.15 -1.19 2.15 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.33}{0.0}{0.0}{0.33}{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 11.99 0 C 11.99 1.19 11.03 2.15 9.84 2.15 C 8.65 2.15 7.69 1.19 7.69 0 C 7.69 -1.19 8.65 -2.15 9.84 -2.15 C 11.03 -2.15 11.99 -1.19 11.99 0 Z M 9.84 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.33}{0.0}{0.0}{0.33}{7.11319pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.24 L 0 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.84 2.24 L 9.84 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{7.11319pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\in\mathcal{C}
by definition. Thus, once more there are simplifications.

\square

Condition P3 is trivially satisfied with respect to any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and will thus be ignored.

\square

We already know from Lemma
6.19

(
b
)

that
A(\mathcal{C},v)
implies the existence of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda I
is small.

(i)
As the first case, let
\mathcal{C}
be both class
\mathsf{NNSB}
and class
\mathsf{NP}
. Since
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is already known to require the existence of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda I
is small, only the converse implication needs proving.

Suppose that
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
is such that
v-\lambda I
is small and let
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be arbitrary. By Lemma
5.2

(
a
)

, then
\lambda=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{h(\mathsf{A}),s}=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{s,h(\mathsf{A})}
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
. Hence, and because
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=0
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
with
2\leq|\mathsf{A}|
by
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NNSB}
, in order to satisfy condition P1 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
the term
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda
has to vanish. And, of course, it does vanish since
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NP}
ensures
0=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})
, where the last step is due to
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NNSB}
. That
\mathcal{C}
is class
\mathsf{NNSB}
also prohibits the existence of
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
for any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
, rendering condition P2 vacuous. Hence,
A(\mathcal{C},v)
holds.

(ii)
Next, suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is class
\mathsf{NNSB}
but not class
\mathsf{NP}
. Here, both implications need to be shown and are addressed individually.

Let
v
be small and let
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be arbitrary. Then,
\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{h(\mathsf{A}),s}=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{s,h(\mathsf{A})}=0
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
. For that reason, the first sum on the left-hand side of the equation in condition P1 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
vanishes. And since
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NNSB}
means
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=0
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
with
2\leq|\mathsf{A}|
the second term does as well. Hence, condition P1 is satisfied. The assumption that
\mathcal{C}
is class
\mathsf{NNSB}
and thus
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})=0
for any
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
|\mathsf{Y}|=2
also implies that condition P2 is trivially fulfilled. Hence,
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is true.

Conversely, because
\mathcal{C}
is not class
\mathsf{NP}
we find some
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
with
t:=|\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}|\neq 0
. By
Lemma
3.11

(
e
)

that necessitates
\{\hbox to5.2pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.15 0 C 2.15 1.19 1.19 2.15 0 2.15 C -1.19 2.15 -2.15 1.19 -2.15 0 C -2.15 -1.19 -1.19 -2.15 0 -2.15 C 1.19 -2.15 2.15 -1.19 2.15 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.33}{0.0}{0.0}{0.33}{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\otimes t},\hbox to5.2pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.15 0 C 2.15 1.19 1.19 2.15 0 2.15 C -1.19 2.15 -2.15 1.19 -2.15 0 C -2.15 -1.19 -1.19 -2.15 0 -2.15 C 1.19 -2.15 2.15 -1.19 2.15 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.33}{0.0}{0.0}{0.33}{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.24 L 0 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\otimes t}\}\in\mathcal{C}
. Hence,
A(\mathcal{C},v)
requires
v
to be small by Lemma
6.19

(
c
)

.

(iii)
Now, let
\mathcal{C}
not be class
\mathsf{NNSB}
but class
\mathsf{NP}
.

If
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
is such that
v-\lambda I
is both skew-symmetric and small, then given any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
, we infer for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
, first,
\lambda=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{h(\mathsf{A}),s}=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{s,h(\mathsf{A})}
by Lemma
5.2

(
a
)

and, second,
\lambda=v_{h(\mathsf{A}),h(\mathsf{A})}
by Lemma
5.2

(
b
)

. Consequently, condition P1 is satisfied with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
if and only if the term
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\,\lambda
is zero, which, of course, it is since
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NP}
guarantees
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=0
. Because Lemma
5.2

(
b
)

also tells us that
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
, condition P2 is met, irrespective of whether there actually is some
\mathsf{B}\in p
with
|\mathsf{B}|=2
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{B})\neq 0
. Thus,
A(\mathcal{C},v)
.

Conversely, if
A(\mathcal{C},v)
, then by the initial remark there exists
\lambda_{1}\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda_{1}I
is small. Additionally, since
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{B}
and not class
\mathsf{NNSB}
we find some
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
with the property that there is
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
|\mathsf{Y}|=2
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
, which means
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
by condition P2 for
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
.
Moreover, given any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
, if
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
is such that
\mathsf{Y}\mapsto j
and
\mathsf{A}\mapsto i
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p\backslash\{\mathsf{Y}\}
and if
h^{\prime}\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
is constant with value
i
, then, considering that
\lambda_{1}=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{i,s}=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{s,i}
by Lemma
5.2

(
a
)

, condition P1 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
yields the identities
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda_{1}+\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\,v_{j,j}+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|\,\wedge\,\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{Y}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=0
for
h
and
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda_{1}+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=0
for
h^{\prime}
. Subtracting the second from the first produces the identity
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})(v_{j,j}-v_{i,i})=0
. Since
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
we can infer
v_{j,j}=v_{i,i}
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
.
By Lemma
5.2

(
b
)

, we have thus shown that there exists
\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda_{2}I
is skew-symmetric. According to Lemma
5.2

(
c
)

, that is all we needed to see.

(iv)
As the final case let
\mathcal{C}
be neither class
\mathsf{NNSB}
nor class
\mathsf{NP}
.

If
v
is skew-symmetric and small and if
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
are arbitrary, then by definition,
\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{h(\mathsf{A}),s}=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{s,h(\mathsf{A})}=0
and
v_{h(\mathsf{A}),h(\mathsf{A})}=0
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
. For that reason, condition P1 is trivially satisfied with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
. Because also
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
condition P2 is met as well, no matter whether there exists
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
|\mathsf{Y}|=2
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
. Hence,
A(\mathcal{C},v)
has been proved.

In order to prove the converse, let
A(\mathcal{C},v)
hold. Since
\mathcal{C}
is not class
\mathsf{NP}
we find a
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
with
t:=|\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}|\neq 0
. As
\hbox to12.31pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\{\hbox to5.2pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.24 L 0 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\otimes t},\hbox to5.2pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.15 0 C 2.15 1.19 1.19 2.15 0 2.15 C -1.19 2.15 -2.15 1.19 -2.15 0 C -2.15 -1.19 -1.19 -2.15 0 -2.15 C 1.19 -2.15 2.15 -1.19 2.15 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.33}{0.0}{0.0}{0.33}{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.24 L 0 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\otimes t}\}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
by Lemma
3.11

(
e
)

. It follows that
v
is small by Lemma
6.19

(
c
)

. Furthermore, the assumption of
\mathcal{C}
not being class
\mathsf{NNSB}
implies the existence of
(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},q)\in\mathcal{C}
and
\mathsf{Y}\in q
with
2\leq|\mathsf{Y}|
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{b}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
. If now for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
the mapping
h\colon q\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
is such that
\mathsf{Y}\mapsto j
and
\mathsf{A}\mapsto i
for any
\mathsf{A}\in q\backslash\{\mathsf{Y}\}
and if
h^{\prime}\colon q\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
is constant with value
i
, then condition P1 for
(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},q)
implies the identities
\sigma^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{b}}(\mathsf{Y})\,v_{j,j}+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in q\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|\,\wedge\,\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{Y}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{b}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=0
and
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in q\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|}\sigma^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{b}}(\mathsf{B})\,v_{i,i}=0
because
\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{i,s}=\sum_{s=1}^{n}v_{s,i}=0
by
v
being small. Subtracting the second from the first yields
\sigma^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{b}}(\mathsf{Y})(v_{j,j}-v_{i,i})=0
and thus
v_{j,j}=v_{i,i}
by
\sigma^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{b}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
. Because the presence of
\mathsf{Y}
in
q
also ensures
v_{j,i}+v_{i,j}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
by condition P2 for
(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},q)
we have thus shown that there exists
\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda_{2}I
is skew-symmetric by Lemma
5.2

(
b
)

. Because
v
is also small, applying Lemma
5.2

(
c
)

(with
\lambda_{1}=0
) we see that
\lambda_{2}=0
, i.e., that
v
is skew-symmetric and small.
∎

Proposition 6.22
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be any category of two-colored partitions and
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.
If
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{H}
and

(i)

class
\mathsf{NNSB}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to
v
being diagonal.

(ii)

not class
\mathsf{NNSB}
but class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to there existing
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v=\lambda I
.

(iii)

not class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to
v=0
.

As
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{H}
, both
