\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{{}}{}{{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{}{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.24 L 0 9.84 L 19.67 9.84 L 19.67 2.24}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.56 2.24 L 6.56 9.84}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 13.11 2.24 L 13.11 9.84}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\in\mathcal{C}
and
2\leq|\mathsf{B}|
for any
\mathsf{B}\in p
and any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
by Lemma
3.11

(
b
)

and
\hbox to12.31pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.15 0 C 2.15 1.19 1.19 2.15 0 2.15 C -1.19 2.15 -2.15 1.19 -2.15 0 C -2.15 -1.19 -1.19 -2.15 0 -2.15 C 1.19 -2.15 2.15 -1.19 2.15 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.33}{0.0}{0.0}{0.33}{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 11.99 0 C 11.99 1.19 11.03 2.15 9.84 2.15 C 8.65 2.15 7.69 1.19 7.69 0 C 7.69 -1.19 8.65 -2.15 9.84 -2.15 C 11.03 -2.15 11.99 -1.19 11.99 0 Z M 9.84 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.33}{0.0}{0.0}{0.33}{7.11319pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.24 L 0 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.84 2.24 L 9.84 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{7.11319pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\notin\mathcal{C}
. Certain simplifications result.

\square

Condition P1 with respect to any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
amounts to the demand that for any
{h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket}
,

\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{h(\mathsf{A}),h(\mathsf{A})}=0.

\square

We already know by Lemma
6.19

(
a
)

that
A(\mathcal{C},v)
implies that
v
is diagonal.

(i)
Suppose first that
\mathcal{C}
is class
\mathsf{NNSB}
. Since it is already clear that
A(\mathcal{C},v)
requires
v
to be diagonal, only one implication needs proving.

If
v
is diagonal, if
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and if
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
, then because
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})=0
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p
by
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NNSB}
the simplified condition P1 of
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
is satisfied trivially. For the same reason, condition P2 is vacuous. And condition P3 is met as well, regardless of whether there is
\mathsf{Z}\in p
with
3\leq|\mathsf{Z}|
, because
v
is diagonal per assumption. Hence,
v
being diagonal implies
A(\mathcal{C},v)
.

(ii)
Next, suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is not class
\mathsf{NNSB}
but is class
\mathsf{NP}
. Now, both implications must be proved.

First, let
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
be such that
v=\lambda I
and let
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be arbitrary. By the initial remark condition P1 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
demands precisely that the term
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\,\lambda
vanish, which, of course, it does because
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=0
by
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NP}
. Moreover, since
v_{j,i}=0
for any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
condition P2 is certainly satisfied, even if there is
\mathsf{B}\in p
with
|\mathsf{B}|=2
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{B})\neq 0
. The assumption that
v
is diagonal also ensures that condition P3 is met, irrespective of whether there exists
\mathsf{Z}\in p
with
3\leq|\mathsf{Z}|
or not. Thus,
A(\mathcal{C},v)
.

Conversely, if
A(\mathcal{C},v)
, then

\mathcal{C}
being not class
\mathsf{NNSB}
lets us find some
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
\mathsf{Y}\in p
with
2\leq|\mathsf{Y}|
and
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
. If, given any
\{i,j\}\subseteq\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
with
i\neq j
we let
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
be such that
\mathsf{Y}\mapsto j
and
\mathsf{A}\mapsto i
for any
\mathsf{A}\in p\backslash\{\mathsf{Y}\}
, then
condition P1 lets us know that
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\,v_{j,j}+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{Y}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=0
. Since
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NP}
implies
0=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,\mathsf{A}\neq\mathsf{Y}}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})
that is the same as saying
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})(v_{j,j}-v_{i,i})=0
, which means
v_{j,j}=v_{i,i}
by
\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{Y})\neq 0
. Hence, if
\lambda:=v_{1,1}
, then
v=\lambda I
as claimed because
v
is diagonal by the initial remark.

(iii) Lastly, assume
\mathcal{C}
is not class
\mathsf{NP}
. Because for
v=0
conditions P1–P3 are trivially satisfied, we only need to prove the converse.

If
A(\mathcal{C},v)
, then by
\mathcal{C}
not being class
\mathsf{NP}
there exists
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
with
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\neq 0
. Hence, for any
i\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
, if
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
is constant with value
i
, then by what was said at the beginning condition P1 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
shows that
0=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\,v_{i,i}
, i.e., that
v_{i,i}=0
. As
v
is diagonal by the same initial remarks, that means
v=0
, as asserted.
∎

Proposition 6.23
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be any category of two-colored partitions and
v\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.
If
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{S}
and

(i)

class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to there existing
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v=\lambda I
.

(ii)

not class
\mathsf{NP}
, then
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is equivalent to
v=0
.

Proof.

In contrast to the situation in the cases
\mathcal{O}
,
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{H}
, there are no general simplifications of the conditions P1–P3 of Lemma
6.18
implied by the assumption that
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{S}
. However, as in case
\mathcal{H}
, since
\hbox to17.72pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.75557pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

it is already clear by Lemma
6.19

(
a
)

that
A(\mathcal{C},v)
holding implies that
v
is diagonal.

(i)
First, let
\mathcal{C}
be class
\mathsf{NP}
.
If there is
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v=\lambda I
and if
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
and
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
are arbitrary, then what condition P1 with respect to
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
demands is that the sum
\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda\allowbreak+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,\lambda=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\,\lambda
vanish. And because
\mathcal{C}
being class
\mathsf{NP}
implies
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}=0
this is indeed the case. Moreover,
v
being diagonal of course guarantees that conditions P2 and P3 are satisfied, no matter what the blocks of
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
are. That proves
{A(\mathcal{C},v)}
.

If, conversely,
A(\mathcal{C},v)
is assumed to hold, then by Lemma
6.19

(
b
)

there exists
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that
v-\lambda I
is small since
\hbox to12.31pt{\vbox to9.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.59998pt\lower-1.75557pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.33}{0.0}{0.0}{0.33}{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{}}{}
{{}{}}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.84 2.24 L 9.84 9.29}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{1.0}{-1.0}{0.0}{7.11319pt}{6.9132pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\in\mathcal{C}
. For any
i\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
the definition of smallness implies
0=\sum_{j=1}^{n}(v_{j,i}-\lambda\delta_{j,i})=v_{i,i}-\lambda
. Hence,
v=\lambda I
, as claimed.

(ii)
The alternative is that
\mathcal{C}
is not class
\mathsf{NP}
.
Of course, if
v=0
, then conditions P1–P3 are trivially satisfied with respect to any
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
.

Conversely, if
A(\mathcal{C},v)
, then by
\mathcal{C}
not being
\mathsf{NP}
there exists
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)\in\mathcal{C}
such that
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\neq 0
. For any

i\in\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
then, if
h\colon p\to\llbracket{n}\rrbracket
is constant with value
i
, then condition P1 for
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d},p)
lets us know that
0=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,|\mathsf{A}|=1}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}+\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p\,\wedge\,2\leq|\mathsf{A}|}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=\sum_{\mathsf{A}\in p}\sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(\mathsf{A})\,v_{i,i}=\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\,v_{i,i}
. Because
\Sigma^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathfrak{d}}\neq 0
that requires
v_{i,i}=0
and thus
v=0
, which concludes the proof.
∎

6.7
Synthesis

Now, we have all the ingredients required to prove the main theorem.

Proof of the main result.

The claims are the combined result of Propositions
6.15
,
6.20
–
6.23
and Lemma
5.3
. More precisely, Lemma
3.11

(
b
)

and

(
c
)

show that
\mathcal{C}
is case
\mathcal{O}
if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is
1\,\wedge\,2
, case
\mathcal{B}
if and only if
1\,\wedge\,\neg 2
, case
\mathcal{H}
if and only if
\neg 1\,\wedge\,2
and case
\mathcal{S}
if and only if
\neg 1\,\wedge\,\neg 2
. Moreover, by definition,
\mathcal{C}
is class
\mathsf{NNSB}
if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is
3
and
\mathcal{C}
is class
\mathsf{NP}
if it is
4
.

The case
2\,\wedge\,3\,\wedge\,\neg 4
cannot occur by Lemma
3.12

(
a
)

. And Lemma
3.12

(
b
)

prohibits the case
\neg 1\,\wedge\,\neg 2\,\wedge\,3
. Hence, the below table covers all possibilities.

Remark 6.24
.

By
[
34
, Proposition 1.4]
, categories of (uncolored) partitions in the sense of
[
4
, Definition 2.2]
can be identified with categories
\mathcal{C}
of two-colored partitions including
. Obviously, such
\mathcal{C}
are never class
\mathsf{NP}
and never class
\mathsf{NNSB}
. The unitary easy quantum groups of
(\mathcal{C},n)
for such
\mathcal{C}
are in particular (orthogonal) easy quantum groups in the sense of
[
4
]
. In combination,
[
3
,
4
,
30
,
31
,
32
,
39
]
provide a full classification of all categories of uncolored partitions, i.e., all orthogonal easy quantum groups:

(a)

There are exactly three case-
\mathcal{O}
categories, giving rise to the
orthogonal group

O_{n}
, the
half-liberated orthogonal quantum group

O^{\ast}_{n}
and the
free orthogonal quantum group

O^{+}_{n}
. For any of these three the first cohomology with trivial coefficients of the discrete dual is given by all skew-symmetric matrices and has dimension
\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)
.

(b)

There are precisely six case-
\mathcal{B}
categories, inducing the
bistochastic group

B_{n}
, the
modified bistochastic group

B^{\prime}_{n}
, the
half-liberated bistochastic quantum group

B^{\#\ast}_{n}
, the
free bistochastic quantum group

B^{+}_{n}
, the
modified free bistochastic quantum group

B^{\prime{+}}_{n}
and the
freely modified bistochastic quantum group

B^{\#{+}}_{n}
. For any one of these the first cohomology of the dual is given by all small skew-symmetric matrices and has dimension
\frac{1}{2}(n-1)(n-2)
.

(c)

Exactly four categories are case
\mathcal{S}
, yielding the
symmetric group

S_{n}
, the modified
symmetric group

S^{\prime}_{n}
, the
free symmetric quantum group

S^{+}_{n}
and the
modified free symmetric quantum group

S^{\prime+}_{n}
. The discrete dual of any of these has vanishing first cohomology with trivial coefficients.

(d)

There are an uncountable number of case-
\mathcal{H}
categories. Among them are categories inducing the
hyperoctahedral group

H_{n}
, the
half-liberated hyperoctahedral quantum group

H^{\ast}_{n}
and the
free hyperoctahedral quantum group

H^{+}_{n}
. Any other case-
\mathcal{H}
category gives rise to either a
group-theoretical hyperoctahedral quantum group

H^{\langle A\rangle}_{n}
(see
[
30
,
31
]
) for some
\mathrm{sS}_{\infty}
-invariant normal subgroup
A
of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\ast\infty}
(such that
A
is neither generated by a single word of length
1
nor a single word of length
2
) or a member
H^{\{\ell\}}_{n}
of an unnamed family of non-group-theoretical hyperoctahedral quantum groups (see
[
32
]
) for some
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
. This includes the quantum groups
H^{(s)}_{n}
of the
hyperoctahedral series
and the quantum groups
H^{[s]}_{n}
of the
higher hyperoctahedral series
, where
s\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
in both cases. Again, the first cohomology with trivial coefficients of the discrete dual of any of these quantum groups vanishes.

Remark 6.25
.

In contrast, the classification of all categories of two-colored partitions and unitary easy quantum groups is still incomplete. Moreover, only a handful of known unitary easy quantum groups have been given proper names. Thus, in most cases, they can only be referenced by their associated categories of two-colored partitions. As explained in Remark
3.9
, it is easy to determine to which of the four cases
\mathcal{O}
,
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{S}
a known category of two-colored partitions belongs and whether it is of class
\mathsf{NNSB}
or of class
\mathsf{NP}
.

(a)

Any known category which is not case
\mathcal{H}
is of the form
\mathcal{R}_{f,v,s,l,k,x}
in the sense of the main theorem of
[
28
]
. For the unitary easy quantum group
G
of
(\mathcal{R}_{f,v,s,l,k,x},n)
, the first cohomology with trivial coefficients of the discrete dual has dimension

\square

n^{2}
if
(f,v)=(\{2\},\{0\})
,

\square

(n-1)^{2}+1
if
(f,v)=(\{1,2\},\pm\{0,1\})
and
s=\{0\}
,

\square

(n-1)^{2}
if
(f,v)=(\{1,2\},\pm\{0,1\})
and
s\neq\{0\}
,

\square

\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)+1
if
(f,v)=(\{2\},\pm\{0,2\})
and
s=\{0\}
,

\square

\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)
if
(f,v)=(\{2\},\pm\{0,2\})
and
s\neq\{0\}
,

\square

\frac{1}{2}(n-1)(n-2)+1
if
(f,v)=(\{1,2\},\pm\{0,1,2\})
and
s=\{0\}
,

\square

\frac{1}{2}(n-1)(n-2)
if
(f,v)=(\{1,2\},\pm\{0,1,2\})
and
s\neq\{0\}
,

\square

1
if
(f,v)=(\mathbb{N},\mathbb{Z})
and
s=\{0\}
and

\square

0
if
(f,v)=(\mathbb{N},\mathbb{Z})
and
s\neq\{0\}
.

Among these are in particular the categories giving rise to the
unitary group

U_{n}
, the
free unitary quantum group

U^{+}_{n}
(see
[
37
,
38
]
) and the three kinds of
half-liberated unitary quantum groups

U^{\ast}_{w,n}
(see
[
1
,
2
,
26
]
and
[
25
, Chapter 3]
) and
U^{\times}_{D,n}
and
U^{{\times}{+}}_{D,n}
(see
[
27
]
and
[
25
, Chapter 3]
and for certain special cases
[
1
,
5
,
6
]
). For any of these, the first cohomology with trivial coefficients of the discrete dual has dimension
n^{2}
.

(b)

Any known category which is case
\mathcal{H}
lies within the scope of
[
15
,
23
,
34
]
or
[
25
, Chapter 1]
. In detail, one obtains for

\square

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{glob}}(k)
of Theorem 7.1 and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{grp},\mathrm{glob}}(k)
of Theorem 8.3 of
[
34
]
dimension
1
if
k=0
and dimension
0
otherwise,

\square

\mathcal{H}^{\prime}{}_{\mathrm{loc}}
of
[
34
, Theorem 7.2]
dimension
n
,

\square

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{loc}}(k,d)
of Theorem 7.2 and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{grp},\mathrm{loc}}(k,d)
of Theorem 8.3 in
[
34
]
dimension
n
if
k=d=0
, dimension
1
if
k=0
and
d\neq 0
and dimension
0
otherwise,

\square

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{hl},\mathrm{glob}}(k,0)
,

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{hl},\mathrm{glob}}(k,s)
,

\mathcal{H}_{\pi}(k,s)
,

\mathcal{H}_{\pi}(k,\infty)
and

\mathcal{H}_{A}(k)
of
[
15
, Table 1]
dimension
1
if
k=0
and dimension
0
otherwise,

\square

any group-theoretical category
\mathcal{C}
in the sense of Definition 4.1.5 of
[
23
]
dimension
1
if and only if
F_{\infty}(\mathcal{C})
as explained in Definition 4.3.21 there contains no word with different numbers of generators and inverses of generators and dimension
0
otherwise,

\square

\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{R}}
of
[
25
, Chapter 1]
dimension
n
.
